### 32. ★★★☆☆ Inside the shadowy world of anti-Putin sabotage from within Russia

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: BBC World
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T00:06:25+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Two men tell of the work they say they have been doing to support Ukrainian intelligence during the war.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Figure caption, Watch: Russian saboteur working for Ukraine shares reconnaissance videos
Two Russian men secretly working for Ukraine&#x27;s intelligence services have given rare accounts of operations they claim to have been involved with, that have targeted military assets and personnel inside Russia.
One of the men, whom we are calling Ivan, claims to have played a role from Russia in Ukrainian drone attacks on strategic bombers and munitions factories on his home soil.
He features in a BBC World Service documentary about the shadowy world of Russian saboteurs.
The documentary, by independent Polish journalist Marcin Mamon, also features a man we are calling Foma, a Russian living in exile in Poland.
Foma runs a network of undercover volunteers in Russia, that surveils high-level officers that the GUR - Ukraine&#x27;s military intelligence service - is considering targeting, he says. He stresses they are never involved in the attacks themselves.
He was also tight-lipped about who they were surveilling, but after Lt Gen Fanil Sarvarov was assassinated in December last year in Moscow , Foma admitted he had provided the Ukrainians with intelligence on him.
Image caption, Lt Gen Fanil Sarvarov was killed in Moscow last year by an explosive device hidden under his car
There have been previous media reports of Russian sabotage groups, but it is rare to get access to someone operating on the ground, like Ivan, explaining alleged activities.
The GUR has confirmed that Ivan and Foma are both working for the agency. But it also has acknowledged that it cannot know the full picture. When Ivan&#x27;s handler was pressed on whether he could be trusted, the handler replied: "I&#x27;m not sure of anyone, except myself."
And both the GUR and the men are cautious about how much detail they share, making it impossible to verify the activities the men claim to have carried out.
The broad tactics Ivan and Foma describe, however, have been acknowledged by the Ukrainian authorities. Kyiv has spoken of its strategy of co-ordinated drone attacks on what it claims are legitimate targets inside Russia, and has acknowledged it uses secret agents for this.
There have also been several political assassinations in Russia since the start of the war. As a matter of policy, Ukraine never officially admits or claims responsibility for targeted attacks, though a Ukrainian intelligence officer has previously admitted to the BBC it was behind the killing of senior military figure Lt Gen Igor Kirillov in December 2024.
It is difficult to confirm how many Russians are doing this kind of work for Ukraine. According to Foma, they number about 300-500, though this is impossible to verify.
If you are outside the UK, watch on YouTube , external and listen to the BBC World Service Documentary Podcast - Shadowmen: Russians working with Ukraine
Ivan and Foma say they are both opposed not just to Russia&#x27;s war in Ukraine, but to President Vladimir Putin&#x27;s regime - and believe his political future depends on the war&#x27;s outcome.
"At the moment... I think the [Putin] regime might fall if they lose the warâ¦ Through our actions... we are trying to speed this up," Ivan says.
His work, he told Mamon, involved surveilling military sites, and locating places to hide drone components, in preparation for potential attacks on Russian assets.
"When these [drone] components are combinedâ¦ it turns out a formidable weapon, which with a single blow can destroy an object that costs many, many millions of dollars," Ivan said.
He sent Mamon a video which showed him apparently hiding explosives near a Russian military airfield.
If he had been caught, he would have been charged with terrorism, and jailed for decades.
Foma&#x27;s work is different - he operates remotely, training his volunteers in Russia, online. Mamon watched him conducting one such tutorial - on how to trail a senior military official marked for surveillance.
Image caption, Foma explained how he taught his undercover volunteers to trail Russian officers
"You will get a file... from me or a colleagueâ¦ with the proposed target. The file will contain [the target&#x27;s] photo, his data, his details, date of birth, age, and a description of him. There&#x27;ll be information on his car, if he has one. When you go to the target, don&#x27;t take a phone," he told his volunteers.
Foma was once part of the Russian elite, so he claims to have a unique set of contacts and constant flow of insider information.
He showed Mamon on his laptop how he mapped military targets.
"This shows a database... the location of storage warehouses, and military units. Where generals liveâ¦ Where the pilots of strategic bombers who shell Ukraine live.
"In order to break the system, you need to understand how it works. Then you can aim for their weak points."
He said there were about "three dozen" people in his network split between Russia, Ukraine and elsewhere in Europe, with the contingent outside Russia providing logistical support, such as tracking targets&#x27; digital footprints.
Foma stressed that the GUR suggests the targets to be surveilled, but that he and his team check for themselves that these individuals should be considered military criminals.
"As a ruleâ¦ we also check the data [on that person]â¦ Only after that we say whether we work or not."
But he argued that his team&#x27;s actions were justified.
"I want people who do terrible things on behalf of Putin and Russia in Ukraineâ¦ to understand that they&#x27;ll have to pay for it. I want them to get scared."
Foma stressed that he only passed on intelligence to the Ukrainians, and his network did not carry out any assassinations themselves.
Of the assassination of Gen Sarvarov, killed in December 2025, he later texted Mamon to say: "Keeping track of him took quite an effort. He lived alternately with his mistress and his colleague. Covered his tracks and constantly changed his schedule."
But Foma did not share any plans with Mamon and the BBC in advance.
Ivan, however, was tight-lipped about the work he had been involved with, even after the event.
After checking with his military intelligence contacts in both Ukraine and Russia, he thought it likely that Ivan had been involved in a plan to strike Russia&#x27;s largest military drone factory at Yelabuga in June last year , external . One person was reported to have been killed in the incident.
Ivan stressed that the aim is always to try to avoid harming civilians.
"The most serious case was when my car was stopped for inspection and I had the handmade explosives on me. The risk was very high - however as you can see, I was not detected," he said.
To mitigate the risk of being compromised, "there is no clear structure" to the work, he said, adding he had never met and did not know the identity of others he had been working alongside.
"As soon as you start gathering people in groups, in general chatsâ¦ leaving a mark on the internet, with a high probability this whole group will be quickly detained or destroyed," he said.
Ivan stressed there were few people left in Russia suitable for this kind of work.
"Most of the ideological people have either left the country or are already behind bars," he said.
Many Russians involved in sabotage work on their native soil are therefore either oblivious - for example lorry drivers unaware that their cargoes have been compromised - or are individuals who have been paid, the BBC understands.
Ivan, on the other hand, says he is doing this work for ideological reasons - he used to be a member of the political opposition. As such, his movements would be expected to be monitored closely by the Russian authorities.
So it was a surprise that he managed to frequently leave Russia and return.
Image caption, Marcin waiting to meet Ivan in Georgia - we were surprised by Ivan&#x27;s apparent freedom to travel
Mamon persuaded Ivan to drive him into the Georgian desert, where they could speak freely - and asked him how the Ukrainians could trust him.
"You go to a third countryâ¦ where [Ukraine&#x27;s] agents meet youâ¦ and conduct lie detector tests," Ivan told him.
Mamon asked Ivan to put him in touch with one of his GUR handlers in Ukraine. This man immediately rang Ivan on speaker phone - apparently demonstrating that Ivan worked for him. They spoke in code, referring to "repairs at the school".
But later, the handler admitted he could not rule out the possibility Ivan might work for the Russians too, either voluntarily or under duress.
Foma thinks the fact that Ivan can travel freely does not necessarily constitute a red flag.
"There are all sorts of holes in [the country&#x27;s governance], and if you know them, or are just lucky, then you can jump through the holesâ¦ and live in the system. Like a mouse in a piece of cheese," he told Mamon.
Perhaps more compellingly, if Ivan had also been spying for FSB, it would seem likely that he would share what he knew of Ukraine&#x27;s attack plans with the Russian agency. More than a year before a devastating strike by more than 100 Ukrainian drones, Ivan had been telling Marcin there would be a major attack on strategic bombers on military airfields across Russia, though he never said when or where.
"Fear is always present," he said. "It follows me everywhere. Even if you don&#x27;t do anything, you&#x27;re still afraid. This is Russia."
But he was optimistic that eventually his life would change.

</details>

### 35. ★★★☆☆ Bank of America Identifies Technoprobe and Soitec as Preferred European Semiconductor Stocks - Yahoo Finance UK

- **分类**: 🇪🇺 欧洲
- **来源**: GN AI
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T08:39:14+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Bank of America Identifies Technoprobe and Soitec as Preferred European Semiconductor Stocks&nbsp;&nbsp;Yahoo Finance UK

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Bank of America Identifies Technoprobe and Soitec as Preferred European Semiconductor Stocks
Semiconductor ©Adobe Stock Images Bank of America has identified Technoprobe (BIT:TPRO) and Soitec (EU:SOI) as its preferred stocks among European small- and mid-cap semiconductor companies, citing their exposure to artificial intelligence infrastructure investment.
The bank's assessment focuses on two areas of semiconductor technology: advanced processor testing and data centre connectivity.
Both companies have received upgrades from Neutral to Buy, reflecting Bank of America's expectations for revenue growth, capacity expansion and improved operating margins.
The analysts expect investment by major cloud service providers in AI computing infrastructure to support demand for the technologies supplied by the two companies.
Bank of America upgraded Technoprobe from Neutral to Buy in May, identifying the Italian semiconductor testing equipment company as having the highest AI-related revenue exposure among the European small- and mid-cap semiconductor companies it covers.
According to the bank, approximately 50% of Technoprobe's current revenue is linked to artificial intelligence applications.
BofA expects this proportion to increase to 70% by 2028, supported by demand for more complex semiconductor testing and developments in high-bandwidth memory (HBM).
Technoprobe supplies probe boards used in semiconductor testing, including products based on vertical microelectromechanical systems (MEMS) technology.
The bank highlighted the company's position in graphics processing unit (GPU) probe boards and expects increasing testing requirements for central processing units (CPUs), application-specific integrated circuits (ASICs), HBM and silicon photonics to support its business.
Bank of America also pointed to Technoprobe's 2026 financial guidance, which it said exceeded median market consensus estimates by 33% for revenue and 65% for EBITDA.
Following the company's guidance, the bank raised its revenue growth forecast for the 2026–2028 period from 40% to 48%.
Its EBITDA growth forecast for the same period increased from 70% to 78%.
These revisions reflect the analysts' expectations for additional demand associated with AI semiconductor testing and the potential contribution from higher-bandwidth memory applications.
The projections remain Bank of America's estimates and are subject to future industry demand and company performance.
Bank of America upgraded French semiconductor materials manufacturer Soitec from Neutral to Buy on October 9.
The bank's assessment centres on silicon photonics, a technology used to support high-speed optical data transmission in computing infrastructure.
According to BofA, Soitec holds approximately 95% of the silicon-on-insulator (SOI) wafer market serving photonics applications.
The analysts expect demand for optical connectivity in data centres to increase as AI infrastructure requires higher data transmission capacity.
BofA believes growth in optical port requirements could exceed growth in AI processor volumes, reflecting the increasing bandwidth requirements of large-scale computing systems.
This trend is expected to support demand for SOI wafers used in silicon photonics applications.
Bank of America expects improved demand visibility to support a potential announcement by Soitec concerning additional manufacturing capacity in Singapore over the coming months.
The bank believes such an expansion could lead the company to revise its financial targets higher.
However, the anticipated capacity announcement remains an analyst expectation rather than a confirmed company decision.
BofA also acknowledged that Soitec's share price has increased substantially during 2026.
As a result, its Buy recommendation is based primarily on expectations for capacity growth and margin expansion rather than the stock's current valuation.
AI Infrastructure Investment Underpins Both Recommendations
Bank of America's selections reflect two different areas of semiconductor infrastructure investment.
Technoprobe provides testing technology for advanced processors and memory devices, while Soitec supplies semiconductor materials used in optical connectivity.
The bank expects demand for both technologies to develop as cloud computing companies expand their AI infrastructure.
For Technoprobe, the principal factors include higher testing complexity, increasing AI-related revenue exposure and projected EBITDA growth.
For Soitec, the assessment focuses on silicon photonics demand, potential manufacturing expansion and the resulting implications for operating margins.
Both recommendations depend on the continuation of investment in AI infrastructure and the companies' ability to deliver the anticipated financial performance.

</details>

Russian Foreign Minister Sergey Lavrov is due in Tehran on Monday for his first visit since the U.S. launched its war with Iran almost eight months ago . He said he will hold talks "aimed at preventing the return of a large-scale war" in the region.
Iran's foreign minister said Sunday that Tehran's proposal for a seven-day ceasefire with the U.S. and a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz &mdash; if the U.S. lifts its naval blockade and sanctions against Iran &mdash; remains on the table after being rejected by President Trump last month.
Last week saw 10 attacks on commercial vessels in and near the Strait of Hormuz, according to the U.K. navy, which said October has become the worst month for attacks on shipping in the strait since the early days of the war in March. Crude oil prices remain over $105 a barrel after hitting a five-month high near $110 over the weekend.
Houthi rebels kill 20 soldiers, Yemen's Saudi-backed military says
Iran-backed Houthi rebels struck a military camp for government forces in southern Yemen on Monday, killing at least 20 soldiers, two Yemeni military officials said.
The rebels fired ballistic missiles at the al-Alam military camp in the southern province of Shabwa in an attack that left at least two dozen more troops wounded, the officials said.
The military officials with the internationally recognized government of Yemen spoke on condition of anonymity because they were not authorized to brief the media.
Yemen's decade-long civil war has intensified and become intertwined with the wider regional conflict sparked by the U.S. war with Iran over the last few months, as the Houthis, considered one of Iran's most potent proxy forces, attacks the government's Saudi backers on their home turf.
Houthi strikes on Saudi infrastructure, including civilian airports, have killed more than 20 people in recent weeks, and President Trump has said he's considering joining Saudi operations against the rebels. The Houthis have warned that if the U.S. does intervene, it would prompt a "swift and painful" retaliation against U.S. personnel and interests across the region.

Oil prices were down early Monday, with Brent crude, the international standard, falling 2.5% to $107.16 per barrel and benchmark U.S. crude dropping 2.2% to $96.84 per barrel.
The prices fell after President Trump late Friday signed an executive order to put new U.S. sanctions against Russia on hold just days before they were to come into effect, drawing disappointment from Ukraine , with the move likely resulting in more energy exports from Russia.
Mr. Trump's decision came not long after he said earlier this month he had reached a diesel deal with Russia in an attempt to bring down elevated U.S. fuel prices ahead of the Nov. 3 midterm elections.
But despite Mr. Trump's controversial efforts to ease energy costs for Americans, oil prices have remained much higher compared to before the Iran war, due in large part to the ongoing risks to the supply chain caused by the crisis in the Middle East .
On Friday the price of Brent crude hit nearly $110 a barrel, its highest level since mid-May.

"The tug has sustained damage but is continuing on its route," the agency said, "one minor casualty has been reported."

U.S. issues security warning to Americans in the Middle East

It added that hostilities have the potential to escalate rapidly and that groups backed by Iran may target U.S. interests overseas "or at locations associated with the United States and Americans throughout the world."

Russia's foreign minister expected in Iran for talks
Russian Foreign Minister Sergey Lavrov was expected to pay his first visit to Iran on Monday since the U.S. and Israel sparked the ongoing war with the Islamic Republic by launching joint strikes on Feb. 28.
Iranian Foreign Ministry spokesman Esmaeil Baqaei said Sunday that the two sides would discuss "regional and international developments," while Lavrov said his visit was "aimed at preventing the return of a large-scale war."
Russian President Vladimir Putin told his Iranian counterpart in early October that Moscow was prepared to contribute to mediation efforts.
Iran presented a seven-day ceasefire proposal in late September that called for an end to the U.S. naval blockade of Iranian ports, the lifting of sanctions on Iranian oil, the reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to direct negotiations on Tehran's nuclear program. President Trump rejected the proposal, which Iran says remains on the table.
https://www.cbsnews.com/live-updates/iran-war-us-trump-talks-russia-strait-of-hormuz/#post-update-78aa481d
Saudi-led military coalition and Iran-backed Houthis trade strikes as Yemen's civil war intensifies
Conflict raged between the Houthis and the Saudi-led military coalition fighting the Iran-backed rebel group on Sunday, as both sides traded strikes.
In Riyadh, where intensifying violence has disrupted life for residents, the landmark Kingdom Centre skyscraper ordered "mandatory" remote working for office tenants until further notice. Although a reason for the order wasn't provided, it followed a security alert and two drone interceptions around the capital city, in addition to a number of attacks by Houthis on Saudi targets in recent weeks.
Meanwhile, the Saudi-led coalition, which backs the internationally-recognized government in war-wracked Yemen, has launched repeated air raids on regions of the country that Houthis control. One alleged Saudi airstrike in northern Yemen killed at least nine people and wounded 16 others on Sunday, the Houthis' Saba news agency reported.
The coalition said Sunday that two pilots were killed when their helicopter crashed in Najran, near the Yemen border, blaming a "technical malfunction" for the incident. The Houthis said they "downed an Apache helicopter belonging to the Saudi enemy" in the same area.
https://www.cbsnews.com/live-updates/iran-war-us-trump-talks-russia-strait-of-hormuz/#post-update-c9ec77fd
Iran says Hormuz reopening conditions still in place
Iran's foreign minister said Sunday that Tehran's conditions to reopen the Strait of Hormuz have not changed, but that its proposal for a limited ceasefire with the U.S. to reopen the vital waterway remains on the table.
Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi told Iran's semi-official Tasnim news agency on Sunday that Tehran is still reviewing "comments" from the U.S. on the Iranian proposal for a seven-day ceasefire that was first presented in September, but rejected by President Trump .
"If they comply with our conditions, the Strait of Hormuz will reopen on the sixth day, and negotiations will begin on the seventh day," Araghchi said, referring to the timeline proposed by Iran, which calls for the U.S. to lift its naval blockade on Iranian ports and to ease sanctions on the country, among other concessions.
"Our conditions are fair, and if the United States is willing to reopen the Strait of Hormuz, it can meet them within a few days," Araghchi said.

### 41. ★★★☆☆ Musk and Anthropic pledge public share giveaways

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: BBC World / BBC Business
- **多源确认**: 2
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T09:38:25+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Musk's announcement comes after Anthropic pledged to create a public benefit fund ahead of its blockbuster stock market listing.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

By Peter Hoskins Â  and Â  Michael Race ,Â  Business reporters
Elon Musk&#x27;s SpaceX and artificial intelligence giant Anthropic have pledged big financial giveaways as Big Tech firms come under pressure over the potential impact of AI on humanity.
Musk said on Monday that he would give one of his shares at his rocket company, which owns xAI, to every child in America next year.
The billionaire&#x27;s announcement came after Anthropic said it would commit 5% of its shares to a fund that will make donations for the "public good".
Anthropic is laying the ground for a potential blockbuster stock market listing that could value the firm at more than $1tn (Â£750bn). SpaceX set the record for biggest initial public offering (IPO) in history in June, raising $85.7bn.
The pledges come at a time when the AI industry is under intense scrutiny over safety concerns and fears about its impact on society and jobs.
Details over how each American child would be handed a share in SpaceX have not been disclosed.
In a post on social media, , external Musk said: "Next year, I will give one of my SpaceX shares to every kid in America. This will be the first of many."
It came after he responded to Anthropic&#x27;s public fund pledge with: "I recommend giving the shares directly to the public. This is what I will be doing."
Anthropic said none of the shares held in its Public Benefit Fund would be used for lobbying or political donations in the US or overseas.
The Claude developer said on Sunday that the fund could also receive another 2% of the firm&#x27;s shares at its board&#x27;s discretion.
Last week, Anthropic told prospective investors that its revenue surged by 45% to $16.8bn (Â£12.6bn) in the three months to the end of September.
In common with other AI firms, it is seeking to position itself as being able to generate huge profits despite spending billions on developing the technology and building infrastructure to power its models.
Companies around the world, primarily technology firms, have cut thousands of jobs after investing in AI, with many pointing to higher efficiencies from its adoption.
Research by the International Monetary Fund (IMF) published in 2024 suggested that almost 40% of jobs around the world will be affected by AI , with that figure rising as high as â60% in advanced economies.
In September, a researcher who quit Anthropic said people working on the technology were "genuinely frightened" about the speed of its advancements and what it could mean for humanity.
"I believe that if we don&#x27;t slow down at the current rate of progress, there is a strong chance that we could all die in the immediate future," he said.
Jacob Coxon was speaking to the BBC after his resignation post on the dangers of AI went viral on a backdrop of growing safety concerns about the industry.
The 27-year-old&#x27;s former boss, Anthropic head Dario Amodei, had previously called for AI development to slow down - though some have questioned the motivations behind this.
The bosses of two rival AI firms, Sam Altman of OpenAI and Musk, have both said they agree with Amodei&#x27;s proposal for industry-wide deceleration and regulation, as well as independent monitoring of AI model development.
Earlier this month, US President Donald Trump and the heads of major AI firms signed a "morally binding" document , making them responsible for ensuring the safety of their own technology.
Trump has repeatedly rejected the idea of laws to control development of the technology despite a debate about the potential risks of AI and what it means for humanity.
Since then, the president has named the director of national intelligence Jay Clayton as the head of a new taskforce on AI which, he said, will co-ordinate government engagement with consumers, companies and "religious organizations".
Why are there concerns AI could threaten humanity, and how real are they?

### 42. ★★★☆☆ Trump says suspension of Russia sanctions is to keep peace negotiations going

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: BBC World
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-18T23:20:15+00:00
- **发生了什么**: The US president blocked numerous sanctions on Russia two days before they were due to take effect.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

US President Donald Trump has said his decision to suspend several Russian sanctions is an effort to keep Moscow at the negotiating table.
"I am in the process of negotiating Peace in the war between Russia and Ukraine," Trump said on his Truth Social messaging platform, adding that he didn&#x27;t want to "do anything that will drive, foolishly, Russia away from the bargaining table".
Trump blocked numerous sanctions on Russia on Friday night, two days before they were due to take effect, as a row with Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky deepened.
Zelensky told NBC News on Sunday that siding with Russian President Vladimir Putin would be a "historic mistake".
Russian envoy Kirill Dmitriev has responded to Trump&#x27;s order by calling it "an important and truly historic moment". He said the sanctions, which were passed by the US Congress and signed into law by the US president in September, would have inflicted "serious damage" on the US and global market.
US Secretary of State Marco Rubio has described the suspension of the sanctions as a 60-day pause during which their potential impact on the US and global economies would be analysed.
In his Truth Social post, Trump also said Zelensky "doesn&#x27;t want to end this war, and probably never did", adding "he could have made a better deal two years ago than he can now".
Zelensky has pushed for an end to fighting, and has repeatedly said that Putin doesn&#x27;t want peace, since Russia launched its full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022.
Relations between Washington and Kyiv frayed after the US struck a controversial deal to import Russian diesel last week.
Despite the international sanctions, Russia has still been able to export oil, much of it to China and India - often at attractive discounts. According to the think-tank Centre for Research on Energy and Clean Air (CREA), China has taken about half of Russia&#x27;s crude exports, with India accounting for about 37%.
In remarks to a European Union summit, after Trump announced the deal to buy diesel from Russia and said Zelensky was "more difficult to deal with" than Putin, Zelensky said it was nice to be among "real leaders".
Image caption, Ukraine&#x27;s President Zelensky attended an EU leaders&#x27; summit in Brussels on Friday
Zelensky has repeatedly pushed for stringent sanctions to limit funding that Russia can use in its war effort. After the latest move from the US, however, he said he believed the Trump administration would lift more sanctions against Russia.
Ukraine and its European allies criticised Trump&#x27;s plans to import Russian diesel to help lower US energy prices, which have skyrocketed during the Iran war.
The deal, according to Zelensky, was tantamount to the US investing in Russia&#x27;s war effort.
Concerns that Trump is siding with Russia have been heightened by disruption to Starlink, a US-provided satellite system used by Ukraine. That hampered Kyiv&#x27;s ability to protect itself from Russian attacks.
Zelensky said US officials had linked speed restrictions to technical issues.
However, he said Ukraine had tested Starlink on EU territory and found it was operating at higher speeds.
Trump has denied both deliberately slowing down the network and a further accusation from Zelensky that the US had stopped sharing defence intelligence.

Oil prices slide as sanctions relief on Russian crude raises hopes of increased supply
Crude oil prices fell Monday as traders weighed possibly higher Russian supplies hitting the market.
U.S. President Donald Trump invoked national-interest to suspend new duties on Russian energy buyers.
Smoke rises above the city following an air attack on the oil terminal in Saint Petersburg on October 18, 2026, amid the ongoing Russian-Ukrainian conflict.
Crude oil prices fell Monday as traders weighed possibly higher Russian supplies hitting the market after U.S. sanctions relief on Friday.
Brent crude futures, the international benchmark, fell 2.38% to $107.25 per barrel. U.S. West Texas intermediate lost 1.78% to $99.60 per barrel.
Ukraine bombing of oil facilities in Russia and more tankers coming under attack in the Persian Gulf, however, have clouded the supply outlook.
Fires broke out at an oil terminal and depot in St. Petersburg after Ukraine launched a massive wave of drone strikes on Sunday, St. Petersburg Governor Alexander Beglov said in a post on Telegram.
The terminal in St. Petersburg exports about 120,000 barrels per day of refined products, mostly fuel oil, according to data from Kpler. It is unclear how bad the damage is at the facility.
Ukraine briefly paused strikes on Russian energy facilities after U.S. President Donald Trump declared on Oct. 11 that Kyiv and Moscow had agreed to a truce .
People watch the burning oil terminal following an air attack in St. Petersburg on Oct. 18, 2026, amid the ongoing Russian-Ukrainian conflict. Russia shot down dozens of Ukrainian drones near St. Petersburg, authorities said, with an oil terminal hit and flights disrupted at one of the country&#x27;s busiest airports.
In the Middle East, meanwhile, two tankers were attacked off the coast of Saudi Arabia in the Persian Gulf on Friday, according to incident reports from the United Kingdom Maritime Trade Operations Centre.
A small boat fired a rocket-propelled grenade at an unidentified tanker 78 nautical miles northeast of Al Jubayl, according to UKMTO. Another tanker, the ST3, was hit by an unknown projectile, UKMTO reported.
Iran has escalated attacks on tankers in the Persian Gulf, Strait of Hormuz and Gulf of Oman in an effort to choke off crude oil exports from the region.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Stock market today: Dow, S&P 500, Nasdaq futures mixed as the AI trade rebounds
Updated Mon, October 19, 2026 at 6:30 AM MDT 1 min read
US stock futures were mixed on Monday, led by a rebound in the technology ( XLK ) sector as investors looked ahead to earnings from Tesla ( TSLA ) and Meta ( META ) this week.
Futures for Dow Jones Industrial Average ( ^YM=DF ) fell 0.1%, while the S&P 500 ( ^ES=F ) gained 0.5%. Contracts for the tech-heavy Nasdaq 100 ( ^NQ=F ) edged up more than 1% after all three major averages ended the week lower on Friday.
The Nasdaq is coming off its worst weekly performance since June, while the S&P 500 is rising after a nearly 2% drop last week.
Semiconductor stocks edged higher, led by shares of Nvidia ( NVDA ) and AMD ( AMD ).
Shares of Nvidia-backed CoreWeave ( CRWV ) were poised to open higher after coming under pressure last week amid AI financing worries.
Tesla will report earnings on Wednesday after the EV maker unveiled a jaw-dropping redesign of the Roadster last week. Social Media giant Meta will also post results on the same day.
Streaming giant Netflix ( NFLX ) will report after the close on Tuesday.
Oil prices were steady with Brent crude ( BZ=F ) at $108 per barrel after Ukraine struck oil facilities in Russia and tankers came under attack in the Middle East. West Texas Intermediate crude ( CL=F ) dropped to $98.
Homebuyers are canceling sales at near-record rates
Homebuyers are backing out of contracts at near-record rates, a potential sign of how higher mortgage rates are prompting buyers to rethink their plans.
Nationwide, 14.5% of homes fell out of contract in September, according to Redfin data, the highest share on record apart from the early months of the COVID-19 pandemic.
Homes can fall out of contract for a number of reasons, such as inspection findings or financing pitfalls, or when a buyer just has a change of heart. But pending sales tend to fall through at higher rates when buyers have more options and more negotiating power, as they currently do in much of the country.
BofA economists maintains likelihood of Fed rate hike this month
BofA economists still see an October rate hike on the table, despite most of the market pricing in an unchanged policy decision for next week, and an increase in December.
The economists point out that the inflation picture has gotten worse since the September meeting. However, 85% of market participants expect policymakers to keep rates steady when they meet next week.
"Most of the FOMC was on board with two hikes this year. Why not do the second one in Oct, to retain the optionality for a third in Dec?" wrote BofA economist Aditya Bhave on Monday.
"We have low conviction as to whether he is serious about bringing a "sea change" to the Fed. But we're giving him the benefit of the doubt, by retaining our Oct hike forecast," added Bhave.

</details>

### 45. ★★★☆☆ Out of Hormuz Energy Shocks, the Ingredients for a Global Food Crisis - Morningstar

- **分类**: 🛢️ 能源
- **来源**: GN Energy
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T11:15:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Out of Hormuz Energy Shocks, the Ingredients for a Global Food Crisis&nbsp;&nbsp;Morningstar

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Out of Hormuz Energy Shocks, the Ingredients for a Global Food Crisis
Out of Hormuz Energy Shocks, the Ingredients for a Global Food Crisis
High costs and shortages of fertilizer and fuel – combined with the looming effects of El Niño – are becoming a threat to global food security, S&P Global Analysis says
NEW YORK , Oct. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Beyond the more well-documented impacts from the disruption of transit through the Strait of Hormuz lies the beginnings of what could be a global food crisis, a new S&P Global analysis  says.
In the first major report  from the S&P Global Institute, authors Daniel Yergin, Shankari Srinivasan, Allan Pickett and Simon Blakey say that the reinforcing shocks of higher energy and fertilizer costs, resilient biofuel-linked demand and El Niño-related weather risk are starting to come into view.
"The world's food producers rely on a steady supply of natural gas and sulfur for fertilizer, diesel for machinery and transport, and open sea-lanes for trade," they write. "Conflict is now testing all of them at once, just as El Niño tests the harvest."
The paper – entitled Energy disruption and El Niño: Leading to a global food crisis?  – points to "pressure building in fertilizer and diesel markets." The article reflects the combined expertise of S&P Global's agricultural and energy research services and research by S&P Global Ratings.
While it has become common knowledge that about 20% of global oil supply and 20% of global LNG trade flowed through the Strait of Hormuz prior to the war, less recognized is the Middle East's role as a major supplier of fertilizer, as well as the feedstocks that other countries rely on to produce it.
Roughly 29% of global trade of urea, the world's most widely used nitrogen fertilizer, normally passes through the Hormuz Strait, as does nearly half of the global sulfur trade and about 21% of the merchant trade of ammonia.
Yet, the fertilizer disruption is only the first channel through which the Iran war reaches global agriculture, the authors say. Fertilizer producers elsewhere depend on energy, mainly natural gas, for feedstock as well as fuel for their manufacture. By restricting oil and LNG flows through the Strait of Hormuz, the conflict also raises fertilizer production costs and farm operating costs far beyond the Gulf.
South Asia is particularly exposed to a double vulnerability of disrupted fertilizer supply from the Gulf and higher costs for the LNG used by domestic producers, the authors note. The Gulf producers are an important source of imported urea for India, while LNG supports around 85% of its domestic urea production. In Bangladesh, the lack of available LNG has resulted in four of the country's five urea fertilizer plants ceasing operation since March 2026.
Diesel Scarcity Tightens the Squeeze on Farm Economics
The global squeeze on diesel – a key operating cost for farmers – extends to those regions that may otherwise be better insulated from immediate fertilizer and LNG shocks.
The Middle East and Russia normally account for half of global net diesel exports. Middle Eastern diesel exports have fallen by more than half since July 2025 to less than 1 million b/d in July 2026. Meanwhile, Ukrainian drone attacks and an export ban have pushed Russian diesel exports from roughly 1 million barrels per day toward zero. China has tightened its restrictions on exports of diesel and jet fuel, also.
With remaining refining capacity already operating near operational limits, diminished supply and low inventories coinciding with seasonal harvest and winter heating demand has taken its toll. The price differential between a barrel of crude oil and a barrel of diesel (historically between $12 and $20) rose above $100 per barrel in the first week of September.
"The conflict has driven diesel prices up much faster than crude oil prices, exposing farmers to a sharp increase in operating costs," the authors write. "Faced with this increase, on top of more expensive fertilizer, farmers in the most affected regions either have to reduce fertilizer purchases and the rate at which they apply fertilizer to their crops, or they may choose to reduce the acreage that they plant. Either way, less food would be produced than would otherwise have happened."
High oil prices also make biofuels more competitive with petroleum, which strengthens demand for the crops and vegetable oils used to produce them. For the first time in recorded history, biofuels became cheaper than oil-based shipping fuels in Europe in September 2026. The resilient demand and policy support for biofuels means that crop markets have less scope to rebalance toward food production.
"Biofuel policy is thus doing more than supporting demand. It is influencing what farmers plant in the middle of an input-cost shock," the authors write.
The anticipated strong El Niño would add further stress just as farmers face higher fertilizer and fuel costs, leaving crops less resilient to heat and disruptive rainfall. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) projects sea-surface temperatures to reach at least 2 degrees C above normal — a threshold reached in only seven episodes since the 1950s.
Brazil's corn output forecast has been reduced by 6 million metric tons to 137 million metric tons, with a further decline of 10 million metric ton possible if El Niño intensifies.
Australia's wheat production could fall to 22 million metric tons, compared with the current forecast of 28 million metric tons.
India's rabi wheat crop faces increased downside risk if monsoon deficits persist; September rainfall was approximately 14% below average.
Brazil's soybean output forecast has been lowered by 6 million metric tons to 177 million metric tons. A model incorporating El Niño severity and reduced phosphate application produces a probability-weighted estimate of 168 million metric tons, with a 65% probability that output falls between 160 million and 170 million metric tons. The effects will be felt globally: Brazilian soybeans are a major feed for Chinese livestock.
Palm oil production in Indonesia and Malaysia faces downside risks of 5.8 million metric tons and 2.5 million metric tons, respectively.
Unlike changes in oil prices, which register immediately, the impact on food will take time to appear. The ultimate extent and severity are yet to be determined, the authors write:
"Farmers buy seed, fertilizer and fuel months before they sell a crop. The squeeze now bearing down on farm budgets may therefore not reach food markets in force until 2027.
"A global food crisis is not foreordained. But neither is it any longer a distant risk. If war continues to impede the movement of energy and fertilizer, and if weather cuts harvests across several major producing regions, the elements of such a crisis will be falling into place."

### 46. ★★★☆☆ Trump endorses embattled Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu for reelection

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: NPR World
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T05:35:39+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Trump said in a post on social media that Netanyahu "has been a Great Warrior Prime Minister, and an even greater Partner for Peace in combatting the Islamic Republic of Iran."

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Trump endorses embattled Israeli PM Netanyahu for reelection Trump said in a post on social media that Netanyahu "has been a Great Warrior Prime Minister, and an even greater Partner for Peace in combatting the Islamic Republic of Iran."
Trump endorses embattled Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu for reelection
FILE - President Donald Trump listens as Israel's Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu speaks at President Donald Trump's Mar-a-Lago club, Dec. 29, 2025, in Palm Beach, Fla.
WEST PALM BEACH, Fla. — President Donald Trump on Sunday endorsed embattled Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu late in his reelection campaign, a coveted boost because of Trump's popularity but one that the American had withheld for months as the vote neared and the leaders' strategies in the Middle East diverged.
Elections in Israel will be a verdict on Netanyahu's handling of October 7
Trump said in a post on social media that Netanyahu "has been a Great Warrior Prime Minister, and an even greater Partner for Peace in combatting the Islamic Republic of Iran."
Israelis are set to vote Oct. 27, in the first national elections since Hamas attacked Israel on Oct. 7, 2023.
The absence of an endorsement was a setback for the Likud politician, who had billed his tight ties to the Republican president as a selling point for staying in power.
"Thank you, President Trump, for the tremendous support and work together," Netanyahu said in a statement in Hebrew posted on X. "Together we will complete the mission!"
His Likud Party attached to the statement a 35-second video showing clips of the two leaders together and Trump praising Netanyahu.
It was not immediately clear what impact the endorsement would have on what is expected to be a tight race. Trump is still popular in Israel, but many Israelis, including leading commentators, have expressed disappointment over the inconclusive outcome of the Iran war and Trump's ceasefire proposal, which fell short of many of the countries' key goals when they jointly launched the war in February.
Trump confirms calling Netanyahu 'crazy,' says they still get along
In his post, Trump again threatened to "soon finish the job" against Iran "unless they change their ways, rapidly," but added he might not want to if Netanyahu loses the election. "If Israel has the wrong Leader, which would be anyone other than Bibi, America will have to do this work ourselves, and I am not inclined to do so," Trump said.
He said Netanyahu had been "Strong, Brave, and Brilliant in the face of tremendous opposition, including that coming from even Israel, itself."
Trump endorsed Netanyahu in past elections and in November he sent a letter to Israeli President Isaac Herzog asking him to pardon Netanyahu on corruption charges.
But Trump's comments about Netanyahu in recent months had been much more subdued, and he repeatedly demurred when asking if he would back the prime minister. The president, when asked in a Time magazine interview published this month about backing the Israeli leader, said, "I'm not involved in politics anymore," despite his campaigning for and endorsing U.S. politicians.
Trump, especially in his second term, has sought to give his stamp of approval to candidates in foreign elections and claimed credit when they have been successful. He endorsed Hungarian Prime Minister Viktor Orbán in his unsuccessful bid for a fifth term, as well as Japanese Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi and Polish President Karol Nawrocki, and several Latin American leaders.
Trump and Netanyahu have been close allies and seemed more aligned than ever in the wake of the joint U.S.-Israeli strikes on Iran's nuclear facilities in 2025. But over the course of the nearly eight-month war with Iran this year, they have had disagreements.
Netanyahu rejects Trump's Gaza Peace Plan, demands Hamas disarm first
Trump criticized Israel's decision to attack a key Iranian gas field in the first weeks of the war. In June, he called Netanyahu "crazy." Trump also said that he was "a little bit perturbed" as Israel's fighting with Hezbollah militants in Lebanon was holding back peace talks with Iran.
Beyond Iran, Netanyahu in August rejected Trump's latest plan to advance a stalled ceasefire in Gaza, pushing back on the U.S. administration's call for Israel to withdraw from territory it controls in Gaza until Hamas has been disarmed.

S&P 500, Nasdaq futures rise as AI stocks rebound ahead of key earnings
Oct 19 (Reuters) - S&P 500 and Nasdaq futures gained on Monday as AI-related shares rebounded from last week's sharp selloff, while a decline in ‌oil prices and Treasury yields provided additional support ahead of key earnings reports from major ‌technology companies this week.
The benchmark S&P 500 and the Nasdaq Composite posted their biggest weekly drop since late June on Friday, as ​concerns that higher interest rates could curb investments in AI infrastructure weighed on technology stocks, particularly data center operators.
The selloff reflected mounting investor unease over how the costly AI buildout will be financed, a question that has increasingly pressured companies underpinning the US bull market, which recently marked its fourth anniversary.
AI-linked stocks such as ‌CoreWeave, which plunged more than 25% ⁠last week, climbed 3.4% in premarket trading on Monday. Chip names also gained, with Broadcom and Micron adding 2.9% and 3.3%, respectively.
Megacap stocks were mixed. While Nvidia ⁠and Amazon rose more than 1% each, Apple and Meta were down marginally.
Focus is now shifting to a busy week of corporate earnings, with Meta expected to beat Wall Street expectations on Wednesday. Elon Musk-led Tesla's results are ​also ​due that day, while SpaceX is scheduled to report ​on Tuesday.
Netflix, General Motors and GE Aerospace are also ‌among the companies reporting results this week.
"What investors really need is stronger monetization, proof that the revenues generated with AI can catch up with the spending on AI tools," said Ipek Ozkardeskaya, senior analyst at Swissquote.
"Because after last week, another promise to spend billions may not sit well in investors' stomachs."
At 07:05 a.m. ET, Dow E-minis were down 73 points, or 0.14%, S&P 500 E-minis were up 36.5 points, ‌or 0.47%, and Nasdaq 100 E-minis were up 307.5 points, ​or 1.05%.
Brent futures were last down 1.3% at around $108.5 a ​barrel, following US President Donald Trump's decision to ​waive some sanctions on Russia. US Secretary of State Marco Rubio said on ‌Saturday the president would take up to 60 ​days to assess the ​economic impact of such a move. [O/R]
Meanwhile, Iran reported another tanker had hit a mine in the Strait of Hormuz, while the cost of shipping and ​insurance was keeping oil prices for ‌actual delivery a lot higher.
Intel shares rose 4.4% after J.P.Morgan upgraded the stock to "neutral" from "underweight."
Cleveland-Cliffs ​gained 13.8% after the steelmaker surpassed analysts' estimate for third-quarter profit.
(Reporting by Tharuniyaa Lakshmi ​and Ragini Mathur in Bengaluru; Editing by Shilpi Majumdar)

### 50. ★★★☆☆ A Repeat Of 'Black Monday' Would Send The S&P 500 Down More Than 1,500 Points And Wipe Out More Than $13 Trillion In Stock Market Value - 24/7 Wall St.

- **分类**: 📈 市场

- **发生了什么**: A Repeat Of &#8216;Black Monday' Would Send The S&#038;P 500 Down More Than 1,500 Points And Wipe Out More Than $13 Trillion In Stock Market Value
Thirty-nine years after a single October Monday vaporized trillions and paralyzed markets worldwide, analysts are drawing uncomfortable parallels between 1987 and today&#039;s AI-fueled rally, and the math behind a repeat scenario is sobering.
A dramati

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

A Repeat Of &#8216;Black Monday' Would Send The S&#038;P 500 Down More Than 1,500 Points And Wipe Out More Than $13 Trillion In Stock Market Value
Thirty-nine years after a single October Monday vaporized trillions and paralyzed markets worldwide, analysts are drawing uncomfortable parallels between 1987 and today&#039;s AI-fueled rally, and the math behind a repeat scenario is sobering.
A dramatic red, burning trend line on a financial chart symbolizes a steep market decline, reflecting the volatility and potential for significant losses discussed in relation to events like &#039;Black Monday&#039;. &copy; Travis Wolfe / Shutterstock.com
The benchmark S&P 500 is still up 11.9% this year, but Wall Street marks Monday, Oct. 19, 2026, the 39th anniversary of the 1987 crash , after a rough week.
On that day in 1987, the Dow Jones Industrial Average suffered its largest one-day percentage drop ever, a 22.6% collapse. But applying Black Monday &#8216;s 20.47% S&P 500 decline to Friday's close would erase about 1,568 points by our arithmetic, leaving the index near 6,092. The Dow's 22.61% drop would remove about 11,750 points, leaving it near 40,245.
This is simple arithmetic on Friday's closes and is not meant as a prediction.
In dollars, the same S&P 500 decline applied to the index's $68.45 trillion market cap at the end of the third quarter, equals about $14.0 trillion.
Friday rattled investors, the S&P 500 fell 1.1% to 7,659.44, the Dow slid 0.3% to 52,002.41 and the technology-packed Nasdaq Composite tumbled 2.1% to 26,140.03. The S&P 500 and Nasdaq logged their worst week since June, led by chip stock. The Dow and Nasdaq remain up 8.2% and 12.5% this year.
The Dow fell 3.81% on Oct. 14, 2.39% on Oct. 15 and 4.6% on Oct. 16. On Oct. 19, it opened at 2,246.74 and closed at 1,738.74, a 508-point plunge. The S&P 500 sank 20.47%, the Wilshire 5000 dropped 18% and estimated worldwide losses reached $1.71 trillion. Systems struggled: 195 of 2,257 NYSE-listed stocks faced delays or halts, and 95 S&P 500 stocks opened late while futures opened on time.
Possible triggers included a House bill to curb tax benefits for mergers and leveraged buyouts and a higher-than-expected trade deficit released Oct. 14. Many saw stocks as overvalued, while trade and budget deficits, rising interest rates and a falling dollar added to concern. Portfolio insurance (computer-driven selling of index futures), index arbitrage through program trading, weekend mutual fund redemptions and early selling ahead of the expected Monday drop amplified the rout. The 1988 Brady Report found margin calls ran about ten times their average size and judged portfolio insurance a possible contributing factor rather than the initial trigger.
On Oct. 20, Fed Chairman Alan Greenspan said the Fed "affirmed today its readiness to serve as a source of liquidity." It injected $17 billion that day, and the federal funds rate fell about half a percentage point. The S&P 500 bottomed at 230.30 in November 1987, down 31.6% from its Aug. 25 high of 336.77, and regained that high on July 26, 1989.
The NYSE adopted trading curbs in 1988, and regulators later created market-wide circuit breakers, used during the 2020 crash. Under the SEC's investor guidance , a single-day S&P 500 decline of 7% or 13% from the prior close before 3:25 p.m. ET halts trading for 15 minutes, with no halt at or after 3:25 p.m. A 20% decline at any time halts trading for the rest of the day. On Friday's close, those levels sit near 7,123, 6,664 and 6,128, so a 1987-size drop would breach the third. Losses can still pile up until each level is hit.
Michael Burry, whose bearish calls we have examined before , wrote in a Substack post, "I continue to believe it is possible the markets are near a major top." He pointed to leverage built by volatility-targeting and momentum strategies in a low-volatility market and compared it with the lead-up to Oct. 19, 1987. He is bearish on AI-linked stocks and reportedly holds short positions including NVIDIA ( NASDAQ:NVDA | NVDA Price Prediction ) and Micron Technology ( NASDAQ:MU ), though he has warned short selling is high-risk.
According to Bloomberg on Oct. 9, Joachim Klement of Panmure Liberum says the AI bubble will burst in 2027 or 2028, with his target implying 36% downside for the S&P 500 by the end of 2027. He is the most bearish of eight strategists Bloomberg tracks; the other seven average about 14% upside. He does not recommend selling now.
The IMF's Global Financial Stability Report on Oct. 13 warned a sharp correction in AI stocks could spread through global markets, citing elevated AI valuations, higher government borrowing costs and a major cyberattack as possible catalysts. "Resilience is not safety," said the IMF's Athanasios Vamvakidis. Temasek CIO Rohit Sipahimalani calls a reversal of the AI trade a key risk.
Fund manager Adrian Day says some conditions mirror 1987, including higher rates, dollar and deficit concerns and an AI sector many consider overvalued, though yields aren't yet high enough to pull large sums out of stocks. Jim Paulsen, on the Excess Returns podcast Oct. 15, highlighted complacency and a possible "fairly nasty correction," particularly in new-era stocks, without a recession or all-out bear market. Riding a mania is one thing; knowing when to leave is the harder half, and we covered both in a free bubble survivor's handbook .
Citigroup ( NYSE:C ) strategists say solid 2027 earnings can support further gains, and Jefferies ( NYSE:JEF ) projects the S&P 500 could reach 9,000 by the end of 2027, per Invezz. The market has historically survived its worst days.
Watch third-quarter earnings and AI capital spending for signs the AI trade is strengthening or weakening.
Contact [email&#160;protected] for any questions or corrections.
AJ spent 10 years writing about financial markets at The Motley Fool. His coverage centers on technology stocks and the broader macroeconomic trends, from interest rates to geopolitics,  that shape where markets are headed next. AJ is drawn to the stories where big-picture economics and individual companies collide.

Stock Market Today: Dow Jones, S&P 500 Futures Rise as Scott Bessent Links September Inflation to &#x27;Energy Shock&#x27; — RCAT, ESLT, STLD in Focus
U.S. stock futures were higher on Monday, as the Dow Jones, S&P 500 and Nasdaq 100 indices rose, following Friday’s lower close.
Energy Secretary Chris Wright defended President Donald Trump on Sunday amid criticism of rising energy costs, saying Trump wants to bring peace and drive energy prices down. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said September’s hotter inflation reading reflects an "energy shock" and argued prices will ease once the U.S. gets "to the other side of the Iran conflict."
Iran’s Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi accused Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu of driving the U.S. into another war, while Reuters reported Vortexa data showing Iran’s seaborne crude exports fell from an average of 230,000 barrels per day to zero in September and stayed there as of Oct. 5.
Investors are preparing for a massive week of third-quarter earnings, with Magnificent 7 members Meta Platforms Inc. META and Tesla Inc. TSLA taking center stage on Wednesday. Other major reports due this week include Netflix Inc. NFLX , General Motors Co. GM , Lam Research Corp. LRCX , International Business Machines Corp. IBM , and Procter & Gamble Co. PG .
Meanwhile, the 10-year Treasury bond yielded 5.07%, and the 2-year Treasury bond yielded 4.72% at the last check. The CME Group’s FedWatch tool projections show markets pricing in a 14.9% likelihood of the Federal Reserve hiking interest rates after its October meeting. –
The SPDR S&P 500 ETF Trust (NYSE: SPY ) and Invesco QQQ Trust ETF QQQ , which track the S&P 500 index and Nasdaq 100 index, respectively, were higher in premarket trading on Monday. The SPY was up 0.53% at $767.33, while the QQQ advanced 1.09% to $717.53.
Disc Medicine Inc. IRON was 17.89% higher in premarket trading on Monday after the company said it will host a webcast on Monday, Oct. 19, to discuss topline results from the APOLLO Phase 3 trial of bitopertin in erythropoietic protoporphyria.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that IRON maintains a negative price trend in the short, medium, and long terms, with a poor momentum score.
Steel Dynamics Inc. STLD was 0.06% higher as its third-quarter 2026 results are due after the closing bell. Analysts expect $5.41 in EPS on revenue of $6.44 billion.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that STLD maintains a positive price trend in the short and long terms but a negative trend in the medium term, with a strong quality score.
Elbit Systems Ltd. ESLT was 0.95% lower despite announcing that it was awarded a contract valued at approximately $235 million to supply EXTRA-MR (Medium Range) rockets and Precise & Universal Launching System rocket artillery systems to a European customer.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that ESLT maintains a negative price trend in the medium term but a positive trend in the short and long terms, with a strong growth score.
Red Cat Holdings Inc. RCAT was 5.30% higher as it announced a voluntary public purchase offer to acquire all outstanding shares of the Austrian engine manufacturer, Steyr Motors.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that RCAT maintains a negative price trend in the short, medium, and long terms.
OpenWorld Inc. O OPNW surged 20.68% in premarket trading on Monday as as it entered into a non-binding term sheet with Meridian for a tokenized financing program of up to $1 billion to support a distributed AI computing network utilizing Tesla Powerwall batteries and solar energy, and recently secured majority stockholder approval for share authorization changes.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that OPNW maintains a negative price trend in the short, medium, and long terms.
U.S. stocks closed lower on Friday, with the Dow down over 100 points. Energy, utilities and consumer staples led, while information technology, communication services and consumer discretionary lagged.
Mohamed El-Erian said the past week delivered an unusual mix in markets, with government bond yields falling despite "hotter-than-expected CPI, PPI, and import price inflation," alongside a drop to under 20% in the market’s implied probability of an October rate hike.
He wrote that equities showed "volatility and dispersion," with tech selling off and the NASDAQ down 4.5% for the week while the S&P 500 fell 2% and the Dow ended the week positive. El-Erian highlighted CoreWeave as a focal point, noting its credit spread spiked to 1,200 basis points and sparked debate on whether it is "the canary in the tech-bubble coal mine" or a "classic market overshoot."
He also cautioned that the "rotation trade" away from tech may not be straightforward, describing a "(1-x) qualification" in which the non-tech sector could be positively correlated with tech’s health.
Here’s what investors will be keeping an eye on this week:
On Monday, October’s NAHB housing market index data will be released by 10:00 a.m. ET.
On Tuesday, September’s housing starts data will be released by 8:30 a.m., and September’s pending home sales index data will be out by 10:00 a.m. ET.
On Wednesday, there are no major economic events scheduled.
On Thursday, initial jobless claims for the week ending Oct. 17 will be released by 8:30 a.m. ET.
September’s leading indicators data will be out by 10:00 a.m., and October’s Kansas City Fed manufacturing survey will be released by 11:00 a.m. ET.
On Friday, October’s S&P flash U.S. manufacturing and services PMI data will be released by 9:45 a.m. ET.
October’s final University of Michigan consumer survey data will be out by 10:00 a.m. ET.
Crude Oil WTI futures were trading lower in the early New York session by 2.12% to hover around $96.92 per barrel.
Gold Spot US Dollar fell 0.17% to hover around $4,177.42 per ounce. The U.S. Dollar Index spot was 0.15% higher at the 101.9639 level.
Meanwhile, Bitcoin BTCUSD was trading 1.40 lower at $82,826.51 per coin.
Asian markets were mixed on Monday, as Australia’s ASX 200, South Korea’s Kospi, and China’s CSI 300 indices rose. India’s Nifty 50 and Japan’s Nikkei 225 indices fell. European markets were lower in early trading. Disclaimer Preview Photo courtesy: Shutterstock
